E[\|\bm{\xi}_{k}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\leq C
for all
k\geq 0
;

(c)

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}[\rho_{k}^{2}+\rho_{k}\|\bm{b}_{k}\|]<\infty
and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\rho_{k}=\infty
almost surely;

(d)

\|\bm{\eta}_{k+1}-\bm{\eta}_{k}\|/\rho_{k}\to 0
almost surely.

Then, we have
\bm{z}_{k}-\bm{\eta}_{k}\rightarrow 0
almost surely.

Lemma A.2

(Adapted from
(
Na and Mahoney 2025
, Lemma B.3)
)
.

Let
\alpha_{k}=\iota_{1}(k+1)^{-p_{1}}
and
\beta_{k}=\iota_{2}(k+1)^{-p_{2}}
be two sequences with
\iota_{1},\iota_{2},p_{1},p_{2}>0
. The following results hold.

(a)

Let
\chi=0
if
0<p_{2}<1
and
\chi=-p_{1}/\iota_{2}
if
p_{2}=1
. Then, as long as
\sum_{t=1}^{l}a_{t}+\chi>0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\prod_{t=1}^{l}\left(1-a_{t}\beta_{j}\right)\beta_{i}\alpha_{i}=\frac{1}{\sum_{t=1}^{l}a_{t}+\chi},

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\left\{\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\prod\limits_{j=i+1}^{k}\prod\limits_{t=1}^{l}\left(1-a_{t}\beta_{j}\right)\beta_{i}\alpha_{i}e_{i}+b\prod\limits_{j=0}^{k}\prod\limits_{t=1}^{l}\left(1-a_{t}\beta_{j}\right)\right\}=0,

where the second result holds for any constant
b
and sequence
\{e_{i}\}
such that
e_{i}\rightarrow 0
.

(b)

If
0<p_{2}<p_{1}\leq 1
, then

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}\left(1-\alpha_{j}\right)\left(1-\beta_{j}\right)\alpha_{i}\beta_{i}=1.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
B\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
A\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times d}
. Suppose
AA^{T}\succeq\gamma_{A}I
,
\|B\|\leq\Upsilon_{B}
for some constants
\gamma_{A},\Upsilon_{B}>0
, and
Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(d-m)}
is a matrix whose columns are orthonormal and form the basis of
\text{Null}(A)
. Then,

Z^{T}BZ\succeq\gamma_{RH}I\Longrightarrow\text{there exists }\delta=\delta(\gamma_{RH},\gamma_{A},\Upsilon_{A})\text{ such that }B+\delta A^{T}A\succeq 0.5\gamma_{RH}I.

Proof.

For any
{\bm{z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we decompose
{\bm{z}}
as

{\bm{z}}={\bm{x}}+\bm{y},\quad\text{where}\quad{\bm{x}}\in\text{Null}(A)\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{y}\in\text{Range}(A^{T}).

(A.3)

Then, we can see that

\displaystyle{\bm{z}}^{T}(B+\delta A^{T}A-0.5\gamma_{RH}I){\bm{z}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aaequ:2}}}}{{=}}\;{\bm{x}}^{T}B{\bm{x}}+2{\bm{x}}^{T}B\bm{y}+\bm{y}^{T}B\bm{y}+\delta\|A({\bm{x}}+\bm{y})\|^{2}-0.5\gamma_{RH}(\|{\bm{x}}\|^{2}+\|\bm{y}\|^{2})

\displaystyle\geq 0.5\gamma_{RH}\|{\bm{x}}\|^{2}-2\Upsilon_{B}\|{\bm{x}}\|\cdot\|\bm{y}\|-\Upsilon_{B}\|\bm{y}\|^{2}+\delta\gamma_{A}\|\bm{y}\|^{2}-0.5\gamma_{RH}\|\bm{y}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=0.5\gamma_{RH}\left(\|{\bm{x}}\|-\frac{2\Upsilon_{B}}{\gamma_{RH}}\|\bm{y}\|\right)^{2}+(\delta\gamma_{A}-\Upsilon_{B}-0.5\gamma_{RH}-\frac{2\Upsilon_{B}^{2}}{\gamma_{RH}})\|\bm{y}\|^{2},

where the inequality follows from
Z^{T}BZ\succeq\gamma_{RH}I
,
\|B\|\leq\Upsilon_{B}
and
AA^{T}\succeq\gamma_{A}I
. Therefore,
B+\delta A^{T}A\succeq 0.5\gamma_{RH}I
as long as
\delta\geq(\Upsilon_{B}+0.5\gamma_{RH}+2\Upsilon_{B}^{2}/\gamma_{RH})/\gamma_{A}
.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs of Section
3

B.1
Proof of Lemma
3.5

We use the objective gradient estimation as an example, while the same analysis applies to the constraint Jacobian. Our analysis is entrywise. Recall that for any vector
v
,
v^{i}
denotes the
i
-th entry of
v
. For any
1\leq i\leq d
, we apply Taylor’s expansion and have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F^{i}({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})-\nabla{f}_{k}^{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:2}}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{F\left(\bm{x}_{k}+b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k}\right)-F\left(\bm{x}_{k}-b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k}\right)}{2b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i}}-\nabla{f}_{k}^{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{f\left(\bm{x}_{k}+b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}\right)-f\left(\bm{x}_{k}-b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}\right)}{2b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i}}-\nabla{f}_{k}^{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\quad\text{(by Assumption \ref{ass:2-1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\mathbb{E}\left[(b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i})^{-1}\sum_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}}\sum_{i_{3}}b_{k}^{3}[\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x}_{k}^{+})+\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x}_{k}^{-})]^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i_{1}}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i_{2}}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i_{3}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right],

(B.1)

where
\bm{x}_{k}^{\pm}
are some points lying on the line segments between
\bm{x}_{k}
and
\bm{x}_{k}\pm b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}
, respectively, and the last equality also applies the symmetry condition on
\bm{\Delta}_{k}
in Assumption
3.3
. By the boundedness of
\bm{\Delta}_{k}
in Assumption
3.3
and boundedness of
\nabla^{3}f
in Assumption
3.1
, we further have

\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F^{i}({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})-\nabla{f}_{k}^{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{aequ:1}}}{{=}}O\left(\frac{b_{k}^{2}}{6}\sum_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}}\sum_{i_{3}}\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i_{1}}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i_{2}}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i_{3}}}{\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i}}\right|\right)=O(b_{k}^{2}).

(B.2)

This completes the proof of the first part of the lemma. Now, we consider objective Hessian estimation, while noting that the same analysis applies directly to the constraint Hessian. For any
1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq d
, we know

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\delta\widetilde{\nabla}F^{\ell_{1}}({\bm{x}}_{k}\pm b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k})}{2b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{\ell_{2}}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{snequ:11}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{\widetilde{\nabla}F^{\ell_{1}}({\bm{x}}_{k}+b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k})-\widetilde{\nabla}F^{\ell_{1}}({\bm{x}}_{k}-b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k})}{2b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{\ell_{2}}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\Bigg].

Applying the definition (
8
) and following the same analysis as in (
B.1
) and (
B.2
), we can have

\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\nabla}F^{\ell_{1}}({\bm{x}}_{k}\pm b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\bm{\Delta}_{k}]=\nabla f^{\ell_{1}}(\bm{x}_{k}\pm b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k})+O(\widetilde{b}_{k}^{2}).

Combining the above two displays and applying the Taylor’s expansion, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\delta\widetilde{\nabla}F^{\ell_{1}}({\bm{x}}_{k}\pm b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k})}{2b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{\ell_{2}}}-\nabla^{2}f^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\nabla f^{\ell_{1}}(\bm{x}_{k}+b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k})-\nabla f^{\ell_{1}}(\bm{x}_{k}-b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k})}{2b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{\ell_{2}}}-\nabla^{2}f^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]+O(\widetilde{b}_{k}^{2}/b_{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\left[(b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{\ell_{2}})^{-1}\sum_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}}b_{k}^{2}[\nabla^{2}(\nabla f^{\ell_{1}})({\bm{x}}_{k}^{+})+\nabla^{2}(\nabla f^{\ell_{1}})({\bm{x}}_{k}^{-})]^{i_{1}i_{2}}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i_{1}}\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{i_{2}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]+O(\widetilde{b}_{k}^{2}/b_{k})

\displaystyle=O(b_{k}+\widetilde{b}_{k}^{2}/b_{k}),

where we abuse the notation
\bm{x}_{k}^{\pm}
in the second equality from (
B.1
) to let it denote some points lying on the line segments between
\bm{x}_{k}
and
\bm{x}_{k}\pm b_{k}\bm{\Delta}_{k}
, and the second equality also applies Assumption
3.3
.
The last equality is due to Assumptions
3.1
and
3.3
. This completes the proof.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
3.6

By Assumption
3.1
, let us denote
\Upsilon_{\nabla f}>0
such that
\|\nabla f({\bm{x}})\|\leq\Upsilon_{\nabla f}
,
\forall{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
. We use
\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}
as an example, while the same analysis applies to
\bar{G}_{k}
. We note that
\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}
satisfies the following relations:

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{snequ:3}}}{{=}}(1-\beta_{k})\bar{\bm{g}}_{k-1}+\beta_{k}\widehat{\nabla}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\nabla}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=\nabla{f}_{k}+O(b_{k}^{2})\quad\text{(by Lemma \ref{lemma:finite difference for gradient})}.

We establish the almost sure convergence of
\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}
by applying Lemma
A.1
. We check the conditions in Lemma
A.1
. Note that condition (a) in Lemma
A.1
is trivially satisfied. For condition (b), we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\nabla}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})\|^{2}\;

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:2}}}}{{=}}\;\left\|\frac{1}{2b_{k}}\int_{-b_{k}}^{b_{k}}\langle\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k}+s{\bm{\Delta}_{k}};\xi_{k}),\bm{\Delta}_{k}\rangle\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{-1}\;ds\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{-1}\|^{2}\cdot~\frac{1}{2b_{k}}\int_{-b_{k}}^{b_{k}}\|\langle\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k}+s{\bm{\Delta}_{k}};\xi_{k}),\bm{\Delta}_{k}\rangle\|^{2}\;ds

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{-1}\|^{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}\|^{2}\frac{1}{2b_{k}}\int_{-b_{k}}^{b_{k}}\|\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k}+s{\bm{\Delta}_{k}};\xi_{k})\|^{2}\;ds,

(B.3)

where the first inequality is due to the Jensen’s inequality. For any
s\in[-b_{k},b_{k}]
, we know from Assumption
3.2
(
23a
) with
r\geq 2
in (
24
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k}+s{\bm{\Delta}_{k}};\xi_{k})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\bm{\Delta}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k}+s{\bm{\Delta}_{k}};\xi_{k})-\nabla f({\bm{x}}_{k}+s\bm{\Delta}_{k})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\bm{\Delta}_{k}]+2\|\nabla f({\bm{x}}_{k}+s\bm{\Delta}_{k})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\{\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla F(\bm{x}_{k}+s{\bm{\Delta}_{k}};\xi_{k})-\nabla f({\bm{x}}_{k}+s\bm{\Delta}_{k})\|^{r}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\bm{\Delta}_{k}]\}^{2/r}+2\Upsilon_{\nabla f}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2(\Upsilon_{m}^{2/r}+\Upsilon_{\nabla f}^{2}).

(B.4)

Combining (
B.2
) and (
B.2
), and applying Assumption
3.3
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\nabla}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\leq 2d^{2}\kappa_{\bm{\Delta}_{2}}^{2}\kappa_{\bm{\Delta}_{1}}^{-2}(\Upsilon_{m}^{2/r}+\Upsilon_{\nabla f}^{2}),

(B.5)

which verifies condition (b). Condition (c) is immediately satisfied under the conditions
p_{2}\in(0.5,1]
and
p_{2}+2p_{3}>1
in (
24
) of the lemma. For condition (d), we note for the Lipschitz constant
\kappa_{\nabla f}>0
that

\displaystyle\|\nabla f_{k+1}-\nabla f_{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{\nabla f}\|\bm{x}_{k+1}-\bm{x}_{k}\|\quad\text{(Lipschitz property)}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\nabla f}\bar{\alpha}_{k}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:acc2},\eqref{snequ:7}}}{{\leq}}\kappa_{\nabla f}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\|\widetilde{W}_{k}^{-1}\|(\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|+\|c_{k}\|).

(B.6)

By Assumption
3.1
and
(
Na et al. 2022a
, Lemma 1)
, there exists a constant
\Upsilon_{K}>0
such that
\|\widetilde{W}_{k}^{-1}\|\leq\Upsilon_{K}
,
\forall k\geq 0
, and also
\|c_{k}\|\leq\kappa_{c}
. Furthermore, we follow the same analysis as in (
B.2
), (
B.2
), (
B.5
), and obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\nabla}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})\|^{r}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{-1}\|^{r}\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}\|^{r}\frac{1}{2b_{k}}\int_{-b_{k}}^{b_{k}}\|\nabla F({\bm{x}}_{k}+s\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k})\|^{r}ds\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}^{-1}\|^{r}\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}\|^{r}\frac{2^{r-1}}{2b_{k}}\int_{-b_{k}}^{b_{k}}\left(\|\nabla F({\bm{x}}_{k}+s\bm{\Delta}_{k};\xi_{k})-\nabla f({\bm{x}}_{k}+s\bm{\Delta}_{k})\|^{r}+\|\nabla f({\bm{x}}_{k}+s\bm{\Delta}_{k})\|^{r}\right)ds\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{r-1}d^{r}\kappa_{\bm{\Delta}_{2}}^{r}\kappa_{\bm{\Delta}_{1}}^{-r}(\Upsilon_{m}+\Upsilon_{\nabla f}^{r}).

(B.7)

Thus, let us define

\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}\coloneqq\max\{\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{-1}\|^{r},2^{r-1}d^{r}\kappa_{\bm{\Delta}_{2}}^{r}\kappa_{\bm{\Delta}_{1}}^{-r}(\Upsilon_{m}+\Upsilon_{\nabla f}^{r})\}.

(B.8)

Then, we know
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{-1}\|^{r}]=\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{-1}\|^{r}\leq\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}
. For any
k\geq 0
, suppose
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k-1}\|^{r}]\leq\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}
, then

\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|^{r}]\leq\mathbb{E}[((1-\beta_{k})\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k-1}\|+\beta_{k}\|\widehat{\nabla}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})\|)^{r}]\\
\leq(1-\beta_{k})\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k-1}\|^{r}]+\beta_{k}\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\nabla}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})\|^{r}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{aequ:B7}}}{{\leq}}\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}.

(B.9)

This shows
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|^{r}]\leq\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}
for any
k\geq 0
. Combining the above display with (
B.2
), and noting that
p\geq 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\|\nabla f_{k+1}-\nabla f_{k}\|^{r}}{\beta_{k}^{r}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{k+1}-\nabla f_{k}\|^{r}]}{\beta_{k}^{r}}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{\nabla f}^{r}(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p})^{r}\Upsilon_{K}^{r}2^{r-1}(\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|^{r}]+\mathbb{E}[\|c_{k}\|^{r}])}{\beta_{k}^{r}}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{\nabla f}^{r}(\frac{\nu_{-1}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p-1})^{r}\Upsilon_{K}^{r}2^{r-1}(\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}+\kappa_{c}^{r})\alpha_{k}^{r}}{\beta_{k}^{r}}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}O\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}^{r}}{\beta_{k}^{r}}\right)<\infty,

(B.10)

where the first equality is due to Tonelli’s theorem and the last inequality is due to
r(p_{1}-p_{2})>1
in (
24
). The above result immediately implies
\|\nabla f_{k+1}-\nabla f_{k}\|/\beta_{k}\rightarrow 0
almost surely, which verifies condition (d). By Lemma
A.1
, we have
\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}-\nabla f_{k}\rightarrow\bm{0}
almost surely.
The same analysis applies to
{\bar{G}}_{k}
, and we complete the proof for the first part of the lemma. For the second part, we know for each sample path, there exists
K^{\star}_{G}>0
such that for any
k\geq K^{\star}_{G}
,

\|\bar{G}_{k}\bar{G}_{k}^{T}-G_{k}G_{k}^{T}\|\leq\min\{\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{G}}}-\kappa_{2,G},\kappa_{1,G}-\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{G}}}\}.

By Weyl’s inequality
(
Horn and Johnson 1985
, Theorem 4.3.1)
, we know
\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{G}}}\cdot I\preceq\bar{G}_{k}\bar{G}_{k}^{T}\preceq\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{G}}}\cdot I
. Since the modification
\delta_{k}^{G}
is introduced to modify
\bar{G}_{k}
to satisfy this condition, we know there is no need to apply
\delta_{k}^{G}
for all
k\geq K^{\star}_{G}
. This completes the proof.

B.3
Proof of Lemma
3.7

We use
{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}
as an example, while the same analysis applies to
{\bar{G}}_{k}
. We decompose
{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}-\nabla f_{k}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}-\nabla f_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:3}}}}{{=}}\beta_{k}({\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})-\nabla f_{k})+(1-\beta_{k})\left({\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k-1}-\nabla f_{k-1}\right)+(1-\beta_{k})\left(\nabla f_{k-1}-\nabla f_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:3}}}}{{=}}\beta_{k}({\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{k};\xi_{k})-\nabla f_{k})+(1-\beta_{k})\{\beta_{k-1}({\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{k-1};\xi_{k-1})-\nabla f_{k-1})

\displaystyle\quad+(1-\beta_{k-1})\left({\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k-2}-\nabla f_{k-2}\right)+(1-\beta_{k-1})(\nabla f_{k-2}-\nabla f_{k-1})\}+(1-\beta_{k})(\nabla f_{k-1}-\nabla f_{k})

\displaystyle=\cdots

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\nabla f_{i})+\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})(\nabla f_{i-1}-\nabla f_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}])

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}(\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\nabla f_{i})\vskip 10.0pt+\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})(\nabla f_{i-1}-\nabla f_{i}),

(B.11)

where we denote
\nabla f_{-1}={\bar{\bm{g}}}_{-1}
in the last two equalities for clarity. We now proceed to derive bounds for each term in (
B.3
). In particular, using the martingale difference property, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}])\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\;\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\right)^{2}\beta_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:B5}}}}{{=}}\;O\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\right)^{2}\beta_{i}^{2}\right)=O(\beta_{k})\quad\text{(by Lemma \ref{technical lemma:2})},

where the last inequality holds since if
p_{2}=1
, we have
2-1/\iota_{2}>0\Leftrightarrow\iota_{2}>0.5
as in (
25
). For the second term in (
B.3
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}(\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\nabla f_{i})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\left\|\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\nabla f_{i}\right\|=O\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}b_{i}^{2}\right)\quad\text{(by Lemma \ref{lemma:finite difference for gradient})}

\displaystyle=O(b_{k}^{2})\quad\text{(by Lemma \ref{technical lemma:2})},

where the last inequality holds since if
p_{2}=1
, we have
1-2p_{3}/\iota_{2}>0\Leftrightarrow p_{3}<0.5\iota_{2}
as in (
25
). For the third term in (
B.3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})(\nabla f_{i-1}-\nabla f_{i})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{i-1}-\nabla f_{i}\|^{2}]}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\left\{\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i-1}\right\}^{2}\right)\quad\text{(by the same analysis of \eqref{aequ:4}, \eqref{aequ:B7}, \eqref{aequ:B8}, \eqref{aequ:B9})}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}^{2}}{\beta_{k}^{2}}\right)\quad\text{(by Lemma \ref{technical lemma:2})},

where we set
\alpha_{-1}=\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{-1}-\nabla f_{0}\|
in the last inequality and the last equality holds since
p_{1}>p_{2}
, and if
p_{2}=1
,
1-(p_{1}-p_{2})/\iota_{2}>0\Leftrightarrow p_{1}<p_{2}+\iota_{2}
as in (
25
). Combining the above three displays with (
B.3
), we know
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}-\nabla f_{k}\|^{2}]=O(\beta_{k}+b_{k}^{4}+\alpha_{k}^{2}/\beta_{k}^{2})
. The same analysis applies to
{\bar{G}}_{k}
, thereby completing the proof.

B.4
Proof of Lemma
3.8

Let
k\geq 0
. For the result (a), we note that

\widetilde{G}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{decomposition}}}{{=}}\widetilde{G}_{k}(\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{decomposition}}}{{=}}\widetilde{G}_{k}\bm{v}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:acc2}}}{{=}}-c_{k}.

We apply Assumption
3.1
and have
\bm{v}_{k}=-\widetilde{G}_{k}^{T}(\widetilde{G}_{k}\widetilde{G}_{k}^{T})^{-1}c_{k}
. Thus, we obtain

\|\bm{v}_{k}\|\leq\|\widetilde{G}_{k}^{T}(\widetilde{G}_{k}\widetilde{G}_{k}^{T})^{-1}\|\|c_{k}\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{G}}}}}\|c_{k}\|\quad\text{ and }\quad\|\bm{v}_{k}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{{\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{G}}}}}\|c_{k}\|^{2}\leq\frac{\kappa_{c}}{{\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{G}}}}}\|c_{k}\|.

(B.12)

Thus, (a) holds with
\kappa_{v}=\max\{1/\sqrt{\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{G}}}},\kappa_{c}/{\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{G}}}}\}
. For the result (b), we note that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{decomposition}}}}{{=}}\bm{u}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}+2\bm{u}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\bm{v}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\bm{v}_{k}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}\|\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}-2\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}\|\bm{u}_{k}\|\|\bm{v}_{k}\|-\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}\|\bm{v}_{k}\|^{2}\quad\text{(by Assumption \ref{ass:1-1})}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}-\frac{2\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}}{\sqrt{\kappa_{u}}}-\frac{\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}}{\kappa_{u}}\right)\|\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}\quad\text{(by $\|\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}\geq\kappa_{u}\|\bm{v}_{k}\|^{2}$)}.

(B.13)

Thus, as long as
\kappa_{u}
is large enough such that
{2\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}}/{\sqrt{\kappa_{u}}}+{\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}}/{\kappa_{u}}\leq{\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}/}{2}
, the result (b) holds. For the result (c), we note that

\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};\tau_{k},\bm{x}_{k},\bar{\bm{g}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{reduction}}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{2}\tau_{k}\max\{\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k},0\}+\sigma\|c_{k}\|.

If
\|\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}\geq\kappa_{u}\|\bm{v}_{k}\|^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};\tau_{k},\bm{x}_{k},\bar{\bm{g}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:7}}}}{{\geq}}\;\frac{1}{4}\tau_{k}\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}\|\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}+\sigma\|c_{k}\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\tau_{k}\kappa_{u}\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}}{4(1+\kappa_{u})}(\|\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}_{k}\|^{2})+\sigma\|c_{k}\|\quad\text{(by $\|\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}\geq\kappa_{u}\|\bm{v}_{k}\|^{2}$)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{decomposition}}}}{{=}}\;\frac{\tau_{k}\kappa_{u}\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}}{4(1+\kappa_{u})}\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}+\sigma\|c_{k}\|.

Otherwise, we have

\displaystyle\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};\tau_{k},\bm{x}_{k},\bar{\bm{g}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})

\displaystyle\geq\sigma\|c_{k}\|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:6}}}}{{\geq}}\frac{\sigma}{2\kappa_{v}(1+\kappa_{u})}\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\|c_{k}\|\quad\text{(by $\|\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}\leq\kappa_{u}\|\bm{v}_{k}\|^{2}$)}.

Combining the above two displays, we know (c) holds for
\kappa_{q}=\min\{{\kappa_{u}\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}}/{4(1+\kappa_{u})},\sigma/2\tau_{-1},{\sigma}/\{2\kappa_{v}\tau_{-1}(1+\kappa_{u})\}\}
. This completes the proof.

B.5
Proof of Lemma
3.9

From the update of (
18
), we know
\tau_{k}<\tau_{k-1}
if and only if both
\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+\max\{\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k},0\}>0
and
\tau_{k-1}(\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+\max\{\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k},0\})>(1-\sigma)\|c_{k}\|
. From (
15
), we know

{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+{\widetilde{G}}_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{\lambda}_{k}=-\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}-{\widetilde{G}}_{k}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{k}.

Multiplying both sides by
\bm{u}_{k}^{T}
, we obtain

\bm{u}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}(\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\eqref{decomposition}}}}{{=}}-\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}\bm{u}_{k}.

(B.14)

If
\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\geq 0
, we have for some
\kappa_{\tau,1}>0
that

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+\max\{\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k},0\}

\displaystyle\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{decomposition}}}}{{=}}\;\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}(\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k})+(\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k})^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}(\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k})

\displaystyle\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:8}}}}{{=}}\;\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}\bm{v}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\bm{v}_{k}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;(\|\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}\|+\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}\|\bm{u}_{k}\|)\|\bm{v}_{k}\|+\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}\|\bm{v}_{k}\|^{2}\quad\text{(by Assumption \ref{ass:1-1})}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\kappa_{\tau,1}\|c_{k}\|,

where the existence of
\kappa_{\tau,1}
in the last inequality is due to the boundedness of
{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}
(similar to (
B.9
)), the boundedness of
\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}
(hence
{\bm{u}}_{k}
) in (
B.2
), and Lemma
3.8
(a). Otherwise
\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}<0
, we have for some
\kappa_{\tau,2}>0
that

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+\max\{\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k},0\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{decomposition}}}}{{=}}\;\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}(\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{v}_{k})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:8}}}}{{=}}\;\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}^{T}\bm{v}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k}^{T}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\bm{v}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\;(\|\bar{\bm{g}}_{k}\|+\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}\|\bm{u}_{k}\|)\|\bm{v}_{k}\|\quad\text{(by Assumption \ref{ass:1-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\;\kappa_{\tau,2}\|c_{k}\|,

where the existence of
\kappa_{\tau,2}
in the last inequality follows from the same reasoning as
\kappa_{\tau,1}
. Combining the above two displays, we know that, to have
\tau_{k}<\tau_{k-1}
, we must have
\tau_{k-1}>(1-\sigma)/\max\{\kappa_{\tau,1},\kappa_{\tau,2}\}
. This, combined with the fact that Algorithm
1
decreases
\tau_{k}
by at least a constant factor whenever it is reduced, implies the existence of a (potentially random)
K^{\star}_{\tau}>0
such that
\tau_{k}=\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau}}\geq\widetilde{\tau}=(1-\sigma)(1-\epsilon)/\max\{\kappa_{\tau,1},\kappa_{\tau,2}\}
for all
k\geq K^{\star}_{\tau}
. We now proceed to prove the stabilization of
\nu_{k}
. By Lemma
3.8
(c) and the lower bound of
\tau_{k}
demonstrated above, we have

\nu_{k}^{\text{trial}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\eqref{def:acc6}}}}{{=}}\frac{\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};\tau_{k},\bm{x}_{k},\bar{\bm{g}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})}{\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}}\geq\frac{\kappa_{q}\tau_{k}(\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)}{\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}}\geq\kappa_{q}\tau_{k}\geq\kappa_{q}\widetilde{\tau}.

This, combined with the fact that Algorithm
1
decreases
\nu_{k}
by at least a constant factor whenever it is reduced, implies the existence of a (potentially random)
K^{\star}_{\nu}>0
such that
\nu_{k}
stabilizes as
\nu_{k}=\nu_{K^{\star}_{\nu}}\geq\widetilde{\nu}=(1-\epsilon)\kappa_{q}\widetilde{\tau}
for all
k\geq K^{\star}_{\nu}
. Letting
K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}=\max\{K^{\star}_{\tau},K^{\star}_{\nu}\}
completes the proof.

B.6
Proof of Lemma
3.10

By Assumption
3.4
and noting that
p\geq 1
in (
21
), we know there exists a (deterministic)
K^{\star}_{1}>0
such that
{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}/{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\leq 1
for all
k\geq K_{1}^{\star}
. We further apply Lemmas
3.6
and
3.9
, and know that there exist (potentially random)
K^{\star}_{G},K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}<\infty
such that
\widetilde{G}_{k}=\bar{G}_{k}
,
\tau_{k}=\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}
, and
\nu_{k}=\nu_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}
for all
k\geq\max\{K^{\star}_{G},K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}\}
.
To proceed, we first validate the well-definedness of
(\Delta\bm{x}_{k},\Delta\bm{\lambda}_{k})
. By Lemma
A.3
and Assumption
3.1
, we know there exists
\delta=\delta(\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{G}}},\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}},\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}})
such that
{\widetilde{B}}_{k}+\delta\bar{G}_{k}^{T}\bar{G}_{k}\succeq 0.5\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}I
. Since we have from Lemma
3.6
that
{\bar{G}}_{k}-G_{k}\rightarrow{\bm{0}}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely, there exists (potentially random)
K^{\star}_{2}<\infty
such that
{\widetilde{B}}_{k}+\delta G_{k}^{T}G_{k}\succeq 0.25\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}I
for all
k\geq K^{\star}_{2}
, which implies
{\widetilde{B}}_{k}\succeq 0.25\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}I~\text{in Null}(G_{k})
. This result, combined with
\kappa_{1,G}I\preceq G_{k}G_{k}^{T}\preceq\kappa_{2,G}I
in Assumption
3.1
, implies that
(\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k},\Delta{\bm{\lambda}}_{k})
is well-defined. Furthermore, following the same analysis as in (
B.2
), we have

\|W_{k}^{-1}\|\coloneqq\left\|\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&G_{k}^{T}\\
G_{k}&\bm{0}\end{array}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq\Upsilon_{K}\quad\text{ and }\quad\|\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k}\|\leq\Upsilon_{K}(\Upsilon_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{c}),

(B.15)

where we abuse the notation
\Upsilon_{K}
in the analysis (
B.2
) to denote a common upper bound for
{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}
and
W_{k}^{-1}
, and
\Upsilon_{\nabla f}
denotes the upper bound of
\nabla f
in the analysis of (
B.2
). We now proceed to establish the convergence guarantee for
k\geq K^{\star}\coloneqq\max\{K^{\star}_{1},K^{\star}_{2},K^{\star}_{G},K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}\}
. We have

\displaystyle\phi_{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}(\bm{x}_{k}+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k})-\phi_{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}(\bm{x}_{k})

\displaystyle={\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}f(\bm{x}_{k}+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k})+\|c(\bm{x}_{k}+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k})\|-{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}f(\bm{x}_{k})-\|c(\bm{x}_{k})\|

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\alpha}_{k}{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\nabla f_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+\|c_{k}+\bar{\alpha}_{k}G_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|-\|c_{k}\|+\frac{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{k}^{2}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}\quad\text{(Lipschitz property)}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\alpha}_{k}{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\nabla f_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+\|c_{k}+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\bar{G}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|-\|c_{k}\|+\frac{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{k}^{2}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{def:acc2}}}}{{=}}\bar{\alpha}_{k}{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\nabla f_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+|1-\bar{\alpha}_{k}|\|c_{k}\|-\|c_{k}\|+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|+\frac{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{k}^{2}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{\alpha}_{k}{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\nabla f_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}-\bar{\alpha}_{k}\|c_{k}\|+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|+\frac{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{k}^{2}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}\quad\text{(by $\bar{\alpha}_{k}\leq 1$)}

\displaystyle=\bar{\alpha}_{k}{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+\bar{\alpha}_{k}{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}(\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k})^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}-\bar{\alpha}_{k}\|c_{k}\|+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|+\frac{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{k}^{2}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:10}}}}{{\leq}}-\bar{\alpha}_{k}\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})+\bar{\alpha}_{k}{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}(\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k})^{T}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{k}^{2}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:7}}}}{{\leq}}-\frac{\nu_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}\alpha_{k}}{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})+\left(\frac{\nu_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}\alpha_{k}}{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right){\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\|(\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k})\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\nu_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}\alpha_{k}}{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|+\frac{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\left(\frac{\nu_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}\alpha_{k}}{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)^{2}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}.

Taking conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]
and subtracting
f_{\text{inf}}
in Assumption
3.1
on both sides, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}(\bm{x}_{k}+\bar{\alpha}_{k}\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k})-f_{\text{inf}}\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]\leq\phi_{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}(\bm{x}_{k})-f_{\text{inf}}-\frac{\nu_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}\alpha_{k}}{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}\mathbb{E}[\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\nu_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}\alpha_{k}}{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\left\{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\left(\frac{\nu_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}\alpha_{k}}{{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}}(\bm{x}_{k})-f_{\text{inf}}-\frac{\widetilde{\nu}\alpha_{k}}{\tau_{-1}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}\mathbb{E}[\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\left\{\tau_{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\tau_{-1}\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{\nabla c}}{2}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}],

(B.16)

where the last inequality utilizes Lemma
3.9
. We now derive bounds for each positive conditional expectation term in (
B.6
) so that we can apply Robbins-Siegmund theorem
(
Robbins and Siegmund 1985
)
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\tau_{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\tau_{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|]\quad\text{(by Tonelli's theorem)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\tau_{-1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|^{2}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}]}\quad\text{(by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{nequ:10}}}}{{\leq}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\tau_{-1}\Upsilon_{K}\sqrt{2}(\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}^{1/r}+\kappa_{c})\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|^{2}\right]}\quad\text{(by the same analysis of \eqref{aequ:B9})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p})\tau_{-1}\Upsilon_{K}(\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}+\kappa_{c})\left(\sqrt{\beta_{k}}+b_{k}^{2}+\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\beta_{k}}\right)\quad\text{(by Lemma \ref{final fundamental lemma})}

\displaystyle<\infty,

(B.17)

where the last inequality is ensured by
p\geq 1
, and
p_{1}+0.5p_{2}>1
,
p_{1}+2p_{3}>1
,
2p_{1}-p_{2}>1
, as assumed in (
27
) in the statement of the lemma. Therefore, we immediately have

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\tau_{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\right]<\infty

(B.18)

and hence

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p})\tau_{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f_{k}-{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\mid\mathcal{F}_{K^{\star}-1}\right]<\infty\text{\quad\quad almost surely}.

Following the same analysis as in (
B.6
) and (
B.18
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p})\mathbb{E}[\|G_{k}-\bar{G}_{k}\|\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{K^{\star}-1}\right]<\infty\quad\text{almost surely},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p})^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{K^{\star}-1}\right]<\infty\quad\text{almost surely}.

Combining the above two displays with (
B.6
), we have from Robbins-Siegmund theorem
(
Robbins and Siegmund 1985
)
that

\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{k-1}]\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{K^{\star}-1}\right]\\
=\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}[\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{K^{\star}-1}]<\infty,

(B.19)

which implies
P(\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})<\infty\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{K^{\star}-1})=1
. Since the result holds for any
{\mathcal{F}}_{K^{\star}-1}
, we integrate out the randomness of
\mathcal{F}_{K^{\star}-1}
and obtain

\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})<\infty\quad\text{almost surely}.

Utilizing
\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}=\infty
for any run of the algorithm, we know
\liminf_{k\to\infty}\Delta q({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k};{\tau_{k}},{\bm{x}}_{k},{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k},{\widetilde{B}}_{k})=0
almost surely. Furthermore, by Lemma
3.8
(c) and Lemma
3.9
, we know that
\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)<\infty
almost surely. On the other hand, we note for
k\geq K^{\star}
that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}-\Delta\bm{x}_{k}\|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{def:acc2}}}}{{\leq}}\left\|\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&\bar{G}_{k}^{T}\\
\bar{G}_{k}&\bm{0}\end{array}\right)^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\\
c_{k}\end{array}\right)-\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&G_{k}^{T}\\
G_{k}&\bm{0}\end{array}\right)^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\nabla f_{k}\\
c_{k}\end{array}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon_{K}^{2}\left\|\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{0}&\bar{G}_{k}^{T}-G_{k}^{T}\\
\bar{G}_{k}-G_{k}&\bm{0}\end{array}\right)\right\|\left\|\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\nabla f_{k}\\
c_{k}\end{array}\right)\right\|+\Upsilon_{K}\left\|\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}-\nabla f_{k}\\
\bm{0}\end{array}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Upsilon_{K}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{c})\|\bar{G}_{k}-G_{k}\|+\Upsilon_{K}\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}-\nabla f_{k}\|,

where the last inequality is due to the boundedness of
\nabla f_{k}
(cf. (
B.2
)) and the boundedness of
c_{k}
in Assumption
3.1
. Following the same analysis as in (
B.6
) and applying Lemma
3.7
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(\|\bar{G}_{k}-G_{k}\|^{2}+\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}-\nabla f_{k}\|^{2})\right]<\infty.

The above two displays imply
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}-\Delta\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}]<\infty
and thus,
\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}\|\widetilde{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}-\Delta\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}<\infty
almost surely. With this result and
\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)<\infty
, we have almost surely

\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(\|\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)\leq\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(2\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)+2\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}\|\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k}-{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}<\infty.

Utilizing
\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}=\infty
for any run of the algorithm, we obtain
\liminf_{k\to\infty}(\|\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)=0
almost surely. This completes the proof.

B.7
Proof of Theorem
3.11

Let us consider
k\geq K^{\star}\coloneqq\max\{K^{\star}_{1},K^{\star}_{2},K^{\star}_{G},K^{\star}_{\tau\nu}\}
and define
\bm{\lambda}^{\text{sub}}_{k}=\bm{\lambda}_{k}+\Delta\bm{\lambda}_{k}
. By (
15
), replacing
{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}
with
\nabla f_{k}
and
\widetilde{G}_{k}
with
G_{k}
, we have
{\widetilde{B}}_{k}{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\Delta}\bm{\lambda}_{k}=-{\nabla}f_{k}-G_{k}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{k}
. By Assumption
3.1
, we have

\|{\nabla}f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}\bm{\lambda}^{\text{sub}}_{k}\|=\|{\widetilde{B}}_{k}{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|\leq\kappa_{2,{\widetilde{B}}}\|{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|.

By Lemma
3.10
, we know
\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(\|{\Delta}\bm{x}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)<\infty
; thus,
\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(\|{\nabla}f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}\bm{\lambda}^{\text{sub}}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)<\infty
almost surely. Furthermore, if we define
{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}=-[G_{k}G_{k}^{T}]^{-1}G_{k}\nabla f_{k}
, which is indeed well-defined based on Assumption
3.1
, then

\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)\leq\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}(\|{\nabla}f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}\bm{\lambda}^{\text{sub}}_{k}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)<\infty.

(B.20)

Together with
\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}=\infty
, we know almost surely

\liminf_{k\to\infty}(\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\|^{2}+\|c_{k}\|)=0.

We claim
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\|+\|c_{k}\|=0
, and use
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\|=0
as an example; the same analysis applies to
\|c_{k}\|
. Suppose
\limsup_{k\to\infty}[\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\|>0
. For such a run, we can find a sufficiently small number
\epsilon^{\star}>0
and two infinite sequences
\{m_{i}\}
and
\{n_{i}\}
with
K^{\star}<m_{i}<n_{i}
,
\forall i\geq 0
, such that

\|\nabla f_{m_{i}}+G_{m_{i}}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{m_{i}}^{\star\text{true}}\|\geq 2\epsilon^{\star},\quad\|\nabla f_{n_{i}}+G_{n_{i}}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{n_{i}}^{\star\text{true}}\|<\epsilon^{\star},\quad\left\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\right\|\geq\epsilon^{\star}\quad\text{for }k\in[m_{i},n_{i}).

(B.21)

Then, we have for some (potentially random) constant
\Upsilon>0
that

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\star}

\displaystyle\;\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:17}}}}{{\leq}}\;\|(\nabla f_{m_{i}}+G_{m_{i}}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{m_{i}}^{\star\text{true}})\|-\|\nabla f_{n_{i}}+G_{n_{i}}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{n_{i}}^{\star\text{true}}\|

\displaystyle\;\;=\sum_{k=m_{i}}^{n_{i}-1}\left(\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\|-\|\nabla f_{k+1}+G_{k+1}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{k+1}^{\star\text{true}}\|\right)

\displaystyle\;\;\leq\sum_{k=m_{i}}^{n_{i}-1}\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}-\nabla f_{k+1}-G_{k+1}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{k+1}^{\star\text{true}}\|

\displaystyle\;\;\leq\sum_{k=m_{i}}^{n_{i}-1}(\|\nabla f_{k}-\nabla f_{k+1}\|+\|G_{k}-G_{k+1}\|\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\|+\|G_{k+1}\|\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}^{\star\text{true}}\|)

\displaystyle\;\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:7}}}}{{\leq}}\Upsilon\sum_{k=m_{i}}^{n_{i}-1}(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}),

(B.22)

where the existence of
\Upsilon
in the last inequality is due to the same analysis as in (
B.2
) (note that
{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}
is bounded for any particular run due to Lemma
3.6
and boundedness of
\nabla f_{k}
in Assumption
3.1
). Multiplying both sides of (
B.7
) by
(\epsilon^{\star})^{2}
, we have

(\epsilon^{\star})^{3}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\eqref{aequ:17}}}}{{\leq}}\Upsilon\sum_{k=m_{i}}^{n_{i}-1}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\left\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\right\|^{2},

which implies that

\infty\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=m_{i}}^{n_{i}-1}\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\left\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\right\|^{2}\leq\sum_{k=K^{\star}}^{\infty}(\frac{\nu_{-1}\alpha_{k}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p})\left\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\right\|^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{aaequ:3}}}{{<}}\infty.

Here, the last inequality also uses the fact that
p\geq 1
. This leads to a contradiction. Thus, we obtain
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}\|+\|c_{k}\|=0
almost surely.
By Lemma
3.6
and the definitions of
{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}
and
{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star}
, we have
{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star\text{true}}-{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\star}\rightarrow{\bm{0}}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely, which implies
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}\bm{\lambda}_{k}^{\star}\|+\|c_{k}\|=0
almost surely. This completes the proof.

Appendix C
Proofs of Section
4

C.1
Proof of Lemma
4.4

Recall from the proof of Theorem
3.11
that
{\bm{\lambda}}^{\text{sub}}_{k}\coloneqq{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}+\Delta{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}
, where we use
(\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k},\Delta{\bm{\lambda}}_{k})
to denote the solution of (
15
) but with
{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}
replaced by
\nabla f_{k}
and
\widetilde{G}_{k}
replaced by
G_{k}
. Let us define
\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}^{\text{sub}}_{k}={\bm{\lambda}}_{k}+{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}
. By the proof of Lemma
3.10
, we know for any run of the algorithm, there exists a (potentially random)
K^{\star}<\infty
such that
(\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k},\Delta{\bm{\lambda}}_{k})
is well-defined (note that Lemma
3.6
is applicable since (
28
) implies (
24
)). By (
15
), we note for
k\geq K^{\star}
that

\begin{pmatrix}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&G_{k}^{T}\\
G_{k}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}\end{pmatrix}=-\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f_{k}\\
c_{k}\end{pmatrix}\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad\begin{pmatrix}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&(G^{\star})^{T}\\
G^{\star}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{0}}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}=-\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f^{\star}\\
{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&G_{k}^{T}\\
G_{k}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f_{k}\\
c_{k}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&(G^{\star})^{T}\\
G^{\star}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f^{\star}\\
{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aaequ:4}}}}{{\leq}}\;\Upsilon_{K}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f_{k}-\nabla f^{\star}\\
c_{k}\end{pmatrix}\right\|+\Upsilon_{K}^{2}\|\nabla f^{\star}\|\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{0}}&G_{k}^{T}-(G^{\star})^{T}\\
G_{k}-G^{\star}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\;\Upsilon_{K}(\kappa_{\nabla f}+\kappa_{c})\|{\bm{x}}_{k}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\|+2\Upsilon_{K}^{2}\Upsilon_{\nabla f}\kappa_{\nabla c}\|{\bm{x}}_{k}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\|,

(C.1)

where in the last inequality,
\kappa_{\nabla f},\kappa_{\nabla c}
denote the Lipschitz constants of
\nabla f
and
G=\nabla c
;
\Upsilon_{\nabla f}
is the upper bound of
\nabla f
over
\mathcal{X}
(cf. Appendix
B.2
); and we abuse the notation
\kappa_{c}
from Assumption
3.1
to denote the Lipschitz constant of
c
over
\mathcal{X}
. Note that
\kappa_{c}
always exists since
c
has bounded Jacobian as assumed in Assumption
3.1
.
Thus, we have from (
C.1
) that
{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}\rightarrow{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}
almost surely. Then, we characterize
{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}-\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}
. We have from (
15
) that

\begin{pmatrix}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&{\widetilde{G}}_{k}^{T}\\
{\widetilde{G}}_{k}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}\\
{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}\end{pmatrix}=-\begin{pmatrix}{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}+{\widetilde{G}}_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}\\
c_{k}\end{pmatrix}\quad\;\text{ and }\quad\;\begin{pmatrix}{\widetilde{B}}_{k}&G_{k}^{T}\\
G_{k}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k}\\
\Delta{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}\end{pmatrix}=-\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f_{k}+G_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}\\
c_{k}\end{pmatrix}.

Following the same derivations as in (
C.1
) and applying Lemma
3.6
, we immediately obtain
\|({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k}-\Delta{\bm{x}}_{k},{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}-\Delta{\bm{\lambda}}_{k})\|\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely; thus
\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}-\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}\|\rightarrow 0
. Combining the above convergence results, we know
\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}\rightarrow{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely. Finally, for any run of the algorithm and any
\epsilon>0
, we abuse the notation
K^{\star}
to let
{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\leq 1
and
\|\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
for
k\geq K^{\star}
. Then, we know that, for any
k\geq K^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}\|\leq(1-{\bar{\alpha}}_{k})\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\|\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}^{\text{sub}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=K^{\star}}^{k}(1-{\bar{\alpha}}_{j})\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{K^{\star}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|+\sum_{i=K^{\star}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-{\bar{\alpha}}_{j}){\bar{\alpha}}_{i}\|\widetilde{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{\text{sub}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=K^{\star}}^{k}(1-{\bar{\alpha}}_{j})\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{K^{\star}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|+\epsilon\sum_{i=K^{\star}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-{\bar{\alpha}}_{j}){\bar{\alpha}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=K^{\star}}^{k}(1-{\bar{\alpha}}_{j})\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{K^{\star}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|+\epsilon\{1-\prod_{j=K^{\star}}^{k}(1-{\bar{\alpha}}_{j})\}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{K^{\star}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|\exp\left(-\sum_{j=K^{\star}}^{k}{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\right)+\epsilon,

where the third inequality is due to the second inequality and induction. Noting that
\sum_{j=K^{\star}}^{\infty}{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}=\infty
as
p_{1}\leq 1
, we know there exists
K^{\star\star}\geq K^{\star}
such that
\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{K^{\star}}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|\exp(-\sum_{j=K^{\star}}^{k}{\bar{\alpha}}_{k})\leq\epsilon
for all
k\geq K^{\star\star}
. This implies that
\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\|\leq 2\epsilon
for all
k\geq K^{\star\star}
and we complete the proof.

C.2
Proof of Lemma
4.5

We note that by (
13
),

{\bar{B}}_{k}=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}{\widehat{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}+\prod_{i=0}^{k}(1-\beta_{i}){\bar{B}}_{-1}.

Without loss of generality, we suppose
\beta_{k}\leq 1
for all
k\geq 0
(otherwise, we just consider
k
large enough). We obtain from the above display that

\displaystyle\|{\bar{B}}_{k}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}\|=\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star})+\prod_{i=0}^{k}(1-\beta_{i})({\bar{B}}_{-1}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i})\right\|+\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}(\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\prod_{i=0}^{k}(1-\beta_{i})\|{\bar{B}}_{-1}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}])\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\cdot\|\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\cdot|{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{l}-({\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})^{l}|\cdot\|{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c_{i}^{l}-\nabla c_{i}^{l}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{m}|({\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})^{l}|\cdot\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c_{i}^{l}-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c_{i}^{l}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}])\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{m}|({\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})^{l}|\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\cdot\|\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c_{i}^{l}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\nabla^{2}c_{i}^{l}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\|\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}\|+\prod_{i=0}^{k}(1-\beta_{i})\|{\bar{B}}_{-1}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}\|

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\mathcal{I}_{1}^{k}+\mathcal{I}_{2}^{k}+\mathcal{I}_{3}^{k}+\mathcal{I}_{4}^{k}+\mathcal{I}_{5}^{k}+\mathcal{I}_{6}^{k}+\mathcal{I}_{7}^{k}.

(C.2)

We analyze each term separately. We first present a generic result. For any sequence
e_{i}\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, we have
\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}e_{i}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
as long as
\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\beta_{i}=\infty
(as implied by (
29
)). In fact, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
i^{\prime}>0
such that
|e_{i}|\leq\epsilon
for any
i\geq i^{\prime}
. Thus, for
k\geq i^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}e_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{i^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}|e_{i}|+\sum_{i=i^{\prime}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}|e_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=i^{\prime}}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{i^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{i^{\prime}-1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}|e_{i}|+\epsilon\sum_{i=i^{\prime}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=i^{\prime}}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{i^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{i^{\prime}-1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}|e_{i}|+\epsilon\left\{1-\prod_{j=i^{\prime}}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\sum_{j=i^{\prime}}^{k}\beta_{j}\right)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{i^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{i^{\prime}-1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}|e_{i}|+\epsilon.

Since
\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\beta_{i}=\infty
, we can find
k^{\prime}\geq i^{\prime}
large enough such that
\exp(-\sum_{j=i^{\prime}}^{k}\beta_{j})\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{i^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{i^{\prime}-1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}|e_{i}|\leq\epsilon
for any
k\geq k^{\prime}
. Then, we obtain for
k\geq k^{\prime}
that

\left|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}e_{i}\right|\leq 2\epsilon.

This shows
\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}e_{i}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. With this argument, we study each term as follows.

\bullet
For
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{5}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{6}^{k}
, we know from Lemmas
3.5
and
4.4
that
\|\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\|\rightarrow 0
,
\|\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c_{i}^{l}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\nabla^{2}c_{i}^{l}\|\rightarrow 0
,
\forall 1\leq l\leq m
, and
\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
almost surely (where we use the conditions
p_{3}>0
and
2p_{4}-p_{3}>0
from (
29
)). Thus,
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{5}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{6}^{k}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely.

\bullet
For
\mathcal{I}_{7}^{k}
, we have
\prod_{i=0}^{k}(1-\beta_{i})\leq\exp(-\sum_{i=0}^{k}\beta_{i})\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. Thus,
\mathcal{I}_{7}^{k}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
.

\bullet
For
\mathcal{I}_{3}^{k}
, we provide a deterministic upper bound on
{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c_{i}^{l}
for any
1\leq l\leq m
. In particular, we note from the definition (
9
) that

\displaystyle{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c^{l}_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\{c^{l}({\bm{x}}_{i}+b_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i}+\widetilde{b}_{i}{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}_{i})-c^{l}({\bm{x}}_{i}+b_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i})\}-\{c^{l}({\bm{x}}_{i}-b_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i}+\widetilde{b}_{i}{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}_{i})-c^{l}({\bm{x}}_{i}-b_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{i})\}}{2b_{i}\widetilde{b}_{i}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{-1}{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}_{i}^{-T}+{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}_{i}^{-1}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{-T}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2b_{i}\widetilde{b}_{i}}\int_{0}^{\widetilde{b}_{i}}\int_{-b_{i}}^{b_{i}}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{T}\nabla^{2}c^{l}({\bm{x}}_{i}+s_{1}\bm{\Delta}_{i}+s_{2}{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}_{i}){\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}_{i}\;ds_{1}ds_{2}\times\frac{\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{-1}{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}_{i}^{-T}+{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}_{i}^{-1}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{-T}}{2}.

(C.3)

By the boundedness of
\nabla^{2}c^{l}
over
\mathcal{X}
and Assumption
3.3
, we know there exists a deterministic constant
\Upsilon_{{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c}>0
such that
\|{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c^{l}_{i}\|\leq\Upsilon_{{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c}
for any
i\geq 0
and
1\leq l\leq m
. With this boundedness property and the fact that
{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}^{l}-({\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})^{l}\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, we know
\mathcal{I}_{3}^{k}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely.

\bullet
For
\mathcal{I}_{4}^{k}
, we apply Lemma
A.2
and have

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})^{2}\beta_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c_{i}^{l}-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}c_{i}^{l}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]=O(\beta_{k})\rightarrow 0\quad\text{ as }\;\;k\rightarrow\infty.

(C.4)

Thus, the martingale convergence theorem
(
Hall and Heyde 2014
, Theorem 2.18)
implies that
\mathcal{I}_{4}^{k}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely.

\bullet
For
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{k}
, based on Assumption
4.2
, let us fix any
0<\delta^{\prime}<\delta
and let
K^{\prime}>0
be a deterministic index such that for any
{\bm{x}}\in\{{\bm{x}}:\|{\bm{x}}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\|\leq\delta^{\prime}\}
and for all
k\geq K^{\prime}
, we have
{\bm{x}}+s_{1}\bm{\Delta}+s_{2}{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}\in\{{\bm{x}}:\|{\bm{x}}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\|\leq\delta\}
for any
s_{1}\in[-b_{k},b_{k}]
,
s_{2}\in[0,\widetilde{b}_{k}]
, and
\bm{\Delta},{\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}}\sim{\mathcal{P}}_{\bm{\Delta}}
. Note that such a
K^{\prime}
must exist due to Assumption
3.3
and the fact that
b_{k},\widetilde{b}_{k}\rightarrow 0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}^{k}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}])\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\|{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\|\cdot\bm{1}_{\|{\bm{x}}_{i}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\|>\delta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\prod_{j=K^{\prime}}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\sum_{i=0}^{K^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{K^{\prime}-1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\|{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\|\cdot\bm{1}_{\|{\bm{x}}_{i}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\|\leq\delta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\sum_{i=K^{\prime}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}])\cdot\bm{1}_{\|{\bm{x}}_{i}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\|\leq\delta^{\prime}}\right\|.

The first term on the right-hand side converges to zero almost surely since
{\bm{x}}_{i}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
. The second term converges to zero almost surely since
\prod_{j=K^{\prime}}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\leq\exp(-\sum_{j=K^{\prime}}^{k}\beta_{j})\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. The third term also converges to zero almost surely by following the same derivation as in (
C.2
) and applying Assumption
4.2
to show that
\mathbb{E}[\|{\widehat{\nabla}}^{2}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]
is bounded for
{\bm{x}}_{i}\in\mathcal{X}\cap\{{\bm{x}}:\|{\bm{x}}-{{\bm{x}}^{\star}}\|\leq\delta^{\prime}\}
, thereby obtaining (
C.4
), and then applying the martingale convergence theorem
(
Hall and Heyde 2014
, Theorem 2.18)
. Thus, we conclude that
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{k}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely.

Combining the above arguments of
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{2}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{3}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{4}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{5}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{6}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{7}^{k}
and plugging into (
C.2
), we have shown that
{\bar{B}}_{k}\rightarrow\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
almost surely.
For the second part of the statement, for each run of the algorithm with
k
large enough, we know
\|{\bar{B}}_{k}\|\leq\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}
. In addition, we let
\widetilde{Z}_{k},Z^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(d-m)}
be the matrices whose columns are orthonormal and span the spaces of
\text{ker}(\widetilde{G}_{k})
,
\text{ker}(G^{\star})
, respectively. Then, by Davis-Kahan
\sin(\theta)
theorem
(
Davis and Kahan 1970
;
Pensky 2024
)
and Lemma
3.6
, we know

\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{d-m}}\|\widetilde{Z}_{k}-Z^{\star}Q\|\leq 2\sqrt{2}\|\widetilde{Z}_{k}\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{T}-Z^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{T}\|=\|{\widetilde{G}}_{k}^{T}({\widetilde{G}}_{k}{\widetilde{G}}_{k}^{T})^{-1}{\widetilde{G}}_{k}-(G^{\star})^{T}(G^{\star}(G^{\star})^{T})^{-1}G^{\star}\|\rightarrow 0,

where
\mathcal{Q}_{d-m}
denotes the set of
(d-m)\times(d-m)
orthonormal matrices. Thus, we obtain

\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{T}{\bar{B}}_{k}\widetilde{Z}_{k})=\lambda_{\min}(Q\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{T}{\bar{B}}_{k}\widetilde{Z}_{k}Q^{T})\rightarrow\lambda_{\min}((Z^{\star})^{T}\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}Z^{\star}),

which implies
\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{T}{\bar{B}}_{k}\widetilde{Z}_{k})\geq\kappa_{1,{\widetilde{B}}}
for large enough
k
. This completes the proof.

C.3
Proof of Lemma
4.6

To simplify the notation, we will just fix
\epsilon\in(0,1-0.5/(\zeta\iota_{1})\bm{1}_{p_{1}=1})
and denote
\tau_{k_{0}}=\tau_{k_{0}}(\epsilon)
. We use
\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2},\ldots
to denote generic deterministic constants and may also use
O(\cdot)
to ignore them. However, when they depend on
k_{0}
, we denote by
\Upsilon_{i}(k_{0})
for clarification and do not write
O(\cdot)
. In what follows, we suppose
k_{0}
is large enough (threshold index is deterministic) such that

\frac{\nu_{-1}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}\alpha_{k}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\leq 0.5\epsilon^{5}\quad\;\;\forall k\geq k_{0}.

(C.5)

To prove Lemma
4.6
, we need two lemmas, which are proved in Appendices
C.4
and
C.5
.

Lemma C.1
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
4.6
and suppose (
C.5
), there exist constants
\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2}(k_{0})>0
such that for any
k\geq k_{0}
,

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07182pt\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{z}}_{k+1}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{1-2(1-\epsilon){\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\right\}\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]+\Upsilon_{1}\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k+1}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]\leq\Upsilon_{1}(\beta_{k}+b_{k}^{4})+\Upsilon_{2}(k_{0})\exp\left(-\frac{2\iota_{2}k^{1-p_{2}}}{1-p_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 136.5733pt+\Upsilon_{1}\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i}\left\{\mathbb{E}[(\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\|^{2}+\|{\bm{z}}_{i}\|^{2})\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>i}]\right\}^{1/2}\right)^{2}.

Lemma C.2
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
4.6
, for any
q\geq 0
, there exists a deterministic integer
\bar{k}_{0}>0
such that for any
k_{0}\geq\bar{k}_{0}
, there exists a constant
\Upsilon_{3}(k_{0})
such that

\max\left\{\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}],\;\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}]\right\}\leq\Upsilon_{3}(k_{0})\left(\beta_{k}+b_{k}^{4}+\left(\alpha_{k}/\beta_{k}\right)^{2q}\right)\;\;\text{ for any }k\geq k_{0}.

By Lemma
C.2
, we choose
q
large enough such that
2q(p_{1}-p_{2})>\min\{p_{2},4p_{3}\}
. Then, we have
(\alpha_{k}/\beta_{k})^{2q}=o(\beta_{k}+b_{k}^{4})
. This completes the proof.

C.4
Proof of Lemma
C.1

By Algorithm
1
, we know for any fixed
\epsilon\in(0,1-0.5/(\zeta\iota_{1})\bm{1}_{p_{1}=1})
and
k\geq k_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|{\bm{z}}_{k+1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{z}}_{k}+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}({\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{k},{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k})\|^{2}=\|{\bm{z}}_{k}-{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}=\|{\bm{z}}_{k}-{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}-{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k})\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{z}}_{k}-{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}^{2}\|{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k})\|^{2}-2{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\langle{\bm{z}}_{k}-{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k},{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon{\bar{\alpha}}_{k})\|{\bm{z}}_{k}-{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}+({\bar{\alpha}}_{k}^{2}+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}/\epsilon)\|{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k})\|^{2}.

(C.6)

For the second term on the right-hand side, we apply the definition of
\tau_{k_{0}}
in (
4
) and have

\|{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k})\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\leq\|{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k})\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\leq\frac{\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}}{\epsilon^{2}}.

(C.7)

For the first term on the right-hand side, we have

\displaystyle\|{\bm{z}}_{k}-

\displaystyle{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}

\displaystyle=(\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}-2{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\langle{\bm{z}}_{k},{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\rangle+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}^{2}\|{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2})\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}

\displaystyle=(1-2{\bar{\alpha}}_{k})\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}(2\langle{\bm{z}}_{k},{\bm{z}}_{k}-{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\rangle+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\|{\widetilde{W}}_{k}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2})\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:def:tau}}}}{{\leq}}(1-2{\bar{\alpha}}_{k})\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\left(\frac{2}{\epsilon}\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}-{\widetilde{W}}_{k}{\bm{z}}_{k}\|+\frac{{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}}{\epsilon^{2}}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\right)\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:def:tau}}}}{{\leq}}(1-2{\bar{\alpha}}_{k})\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}+{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\left(0.5\epsilon\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}+\frac{{\bar{\alpha}}_{k}}{\epsilon^{4}}\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\right)\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:k_0:1}}}}{{\leq}}(1-(2-\epsilon){\bar{\alpha}}_{k})\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}.

(C.8)

Combining (
C.4
), (
C.7
), (
C.4
) and applying (
C.5
), we obtain

\|{\bm{z}}_{k+1}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\leq(1+\epsilon{\bar{\alpha}}_{k})(1-(2-\epsilon){\bar{\alpha}}_{k})\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}+\left(0.5\epsilon^{3}+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\right){\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:7}}}}{{\leq}}\left\{1-2(1-\epsilon){\bar{\alpha}}_{k}\right\}\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}+\left(0.5\epsilon^{3}+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\right)\left(\frac{\nu_{-1}}{\kappa_{\nabla c}}\alpha_{k}+\psi\alpha_{k}^{p}\right)\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}.

This completes the proof of the first part of the result by taking expectation on both sides and setting
\Upsilon_{1}
large enough. For the second part of the result, we apply (
4
) and note that, for
k_{0}\leq k<\tau_{k_{0}}-1
,

\displaystyle{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k+1}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k+1}

\displaystyle={\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k+1}-\nabla f_{k+1}+({\widetilde{G}}_{k+1}-G_{k+1})^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}={\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k+1}-\nabla f_{k+1}+({\bar{G}}_{k+1}-G_{k+1})^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{snequ:3}}}}{{=}}\beta_{k+1}({\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{k+1};\xi_{k+1})-\nabla f_{k+1})+(1-\beta_{k+1})({\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}-\nabla f_{k})+(1-\beta_{k+1})(\nabla f_{k}-\nabla f_{k+1})

\displaystyle\quad+\left\{\beta_{k+1}({\widehat{\nabla}}c_{k+1}-G_{k+1})+(1-\beta_{k+1})({\bar{G}}_{k}-G_{k})+(1-\beta_{k+1})(G_{k}-G_{k+1})\right\}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:5}}}}{{=}}\;\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\left({\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}\left(\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\nabla f_{i}\right)+\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})(\nabla f_{i-1}-\nabla f_{i})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}({\widehat{\nabla}}c_{i}-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}c_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}])^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}+\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}(\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}c_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-G_{i})^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})(G_{i-1}-G_{i})^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}\eqqcolon\mathcal{J}_{1}^{k}+\mathcal{J}_{2}^{k}+\mathcal{J}_{3}^{k}+\mathcal{J}_{4}^{k}+\mathcal{J}_{5}^{k}+\mathcal{J}_{6}^{k}.

(C.9)

We provide the upper bounds for the terms
\mathcal{J}_{1}^{k}
,
\mathcal{J}_{2}^{k}
,
\mathcal{J}_{3}^{k}
, while the terms
\mathcal{J}_{4}^{k},\mathcal{J}_{5}^{k},\mathcal{J}_{6}^{k}
can be proved in the same way by noting that
\|{\bm{\lambda}}_{k+1}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\leq 1/\epsilon
. For
\mathcal{J}_{1}^{k}
, we apply Lemma
A.2
and have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{J}_{1}^{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{J}_{1}^{k}\|^{2}]=\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})^{2}\beta_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})-\mathbb{E}[{\widehat{\nabla}}F({\bm{x}}_{i};\xi_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:B5}}}}{{\leq}}\;O\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})^{2}\beta_{i}^{2}\right)=O(\beta_{k}).

(C.10)

For
\mathcal{J}_{2}^{k}
, we apply Lemmas
3.5
and
A.2
and have

\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{J}_{2}^{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{J}_{2}^{k}\|^{2}]=O\left(\left\{\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})\beta_{i}b_{i}^{2}\right\}^{2}\right)=O(b_{k}^{4}).

(C.11)

For
\mathcal{J}_{3}^{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{J}_{3}^{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})(\nabla f_{i-1}-\nabla f_{i})\right\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{aequ:4}}}}{{\leq}}\kappa_{\nabla f}^{2}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})\|{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}_{i-1}\|\right)^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\quad(\text{Lipschitz continuity})

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\nabla f}^{2}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j}){\bar{\alpha}}_{i-1}\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{i-1}\|\right)^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}.

(C.12)

We separate the sum on the right-hand side into two parts,
i=0
to
k_{0}
and
i=k_{0}+1
to
k+1
. In particular, for the first part, there exists a constant
\Upsilon_{2}(k_{0})>0
depending on
k_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=0}^{k_{0}}\prod_{j=i}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j}){\bar{\alpha}}_{i-1}\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{i-1}\|\right)^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{aequ:B9}}}{{\leq}}\Upsilon_{2}(k_{0})\prod_{j=k_{0}}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j})^{2}\leq\Upsilon_{2}(k_{0})\exp\left(-2\sum_{j=k_{0}}^{k+1}\beta_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon_{2}(k_{0})\exp\left(-\int_{k_{0}}^{k+2}\frac{2\iota_{2}}{(j+1)^{p_{2}}}dj\right)\leq\Upsilon_{2}(k_{0})\exp\left(\frac{2\iota_{2}(k_{0}+1)^{1-p_{2}}}{1-p_{2}}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{2\iota_{2}k^{1-p_{2}}}{1-p_{2}}\right).

(C.13)

For the second part, there exists a constant
\Upsilon_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}+1}^{k+1}\prod_{j=i}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j}){\bar{\alpha}}_{i-1}\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{i-1}\|\right)^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k+1}(1-\beta_{j}){\bar{\alpha}}_{i}\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{i}\|\right)^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j}){\bar{\alpha}}_{i}\|{\widetilde{\Delta}}{\bm{x}}_{i}\|\right)^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{snequ:7}\\
\eqref{equ:def:tau}\end{subarray}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\Upsilon_{3}}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i}\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}\|\right)^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{3}}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i}\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}\|\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>i}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{3}}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>i}]\right\}^{1/2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Upsilon_{3}}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i}\left\{\mathbb{E}[(\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\|^{2})\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>i}]\right\}^{1/2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:def:tau}}}}{{\leq}}\;\frac{2\Upsilon_{3}}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i}\left\{\mathbb{E}[(\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\|^{2}+\|{\bm{z}}_{i}\|^{2})\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>i}]\right\}^{1/2}\right)^{2}.

(C.14)

Combining (
C.4
), (
C.4
), (
C.11
), (
C.4
), (
C.4
), (
C.4
), and noting that
\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k+1}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\|=\|{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k+1}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\|
, we complete the proof of the second part of the result.

C.5
Proof of Lemma
C.2

We prove the statement by induction. Recall that
\epsilon\in(0,1-0.5/(\zeta\iota_{1})\bm{1}_{p_{1}=1})
is fixed and we denote
\tau_{k_{0}}=\tau_{k_{0}}(\epsilon)
. We have
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{z}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}]\leq\epsilon^{4}
and

\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\right]\\
\leq 2\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\right]\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:def:tau}}}{{\leq}}2\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\right]+\epsilon^{2}\right).

Thus, to prove the result for
q=0
, it suffices to show
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}]
is upper bounded. In fact, we note that

\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{L}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}+{\bar{G}}_{k}^{T}{\bm{\lambda}}_{k}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k}\right]\leq 2\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{G}}_{k}\|^{2}\right]\right).

By (
B.9
) we know
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\bm{g}}}_{k}\|^{2}]\leq\Upsilon_{{\bar{\bm{g}}}}
for all
k\geq 0
while the term
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{G}}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}]
can be proved in the same way. Thus, combining the above two displays, we know the result holds for
q=0
. Suppose the result holds for
q\geq 0
, we aim to establish the result for
q+1
. We apply Lemma
C.1
and obtain for some constants
\Upsilon_{1}(k_{0}),\Upsilon_{2}(k_{0}),\Upsilon_{3}(k_{0})>0
that for any
k\geq k_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{k+1}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\|^{2}\bm{1}_{\tau_{k_{0}}>k+1}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt\leq\Upsilon_{1}(k_{0})\left(\beta_{k}+b_{k}^{4}+\left\{\sum_{i=k_{0}}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i}\left(\sqrt{\beta_{i}}+b_{i}^{2}+\left(\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\beta_{i}}\right)^{q}\right)\right\}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt\leq\Upsilon_{1}(k_{0})\left(\beta_{k}+b_{k}^{4}+\left\{\sum_{i=0}^{k}\prod_{j=i+1}^{k}(1-\beta_{j})\alpha_{i}\left(\sqrt{\beta_{i}}+b_{i}^{2}+\left(\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\beta_{i}}\right)^{q}\right)\right\}^{2}\right)
